経済指標は景気回復の勢いがなお鈍いことを示す
内容。ユーロ圏鉱工業生産はおおむね横ばい圏の推
移。製造業の底割れは回避しているものの、内需を
含め景気回復の広がりはなお限定的
欧
州
経済指標は生産・輸出が底堅い一方、内需の弱さ
が鮮明。8月の鉱工業生産は伸びが加速。輸出も
高い伸びを維持。一方、小売売上高はほぼ横ばい、
投資は落ち込みが継続
中
国
消費者物価は政府支援策で伸び縮小も、物価上
振れリスクは残存。日銀は政策金利を引き上げ、植
田総裁は追加利上げ継続の方針を明言。輸出は

堅調も輸入の伸びが上回り貿易赤字は継続
日
本
先週・先々週（9/14～9/28）発表の主な経済指標
半導体や投資関連財の生産は堅調。AI投資の強さが実体面
にも波及していることを確認。企業部門はなお資金余剰を維持。
外部調達も株式中心で、債務調達の増勢は限定的
割高な米国産原油への代替調達で輸入額が増加する中、原
油タンカー運賃も急騰。中東産への調達回帰が進んでも輸入
負担は下がらず、貿易赤字を通じて円安圧力が残るリスク
注目点② 原油輸送費が急騰。貿易赤字長期化で円安圧力も注目点①旺盛なAI投資が生産を押し上げ。資金調達にも余力

（年/月）
米国：生産は底堅く推移。AI・防衛が投資財生産をけん引、半導体増産も寄与
 2026年8月の鉱工業生産は前年比＋1.4％と底堅い結果
― 半導体関連の生産が引き続きけん引。生産能力が2022年対比1.7倍に拡大するなど生産体制の増強が背景。事実、主
要半導体メーカーによる工場新設・増強を受け、MOSロジック（GPU、CPUなど演算用）の量産が本格化
 AI・防衛分野を中心に投資財生産も堅調さを維持
― 投資関連財はAI・防衛が押し上げる形で同＋4.7％と増勢継続。国防・宇宙関連設備は同＋6.1％ と全体を上回る伸び。
地政学リスクの高まりを背景に軍用機やミサイル・防空システムなどの生産体制強化が図られている模様
4

（兆ドル）
資金余剰
米国：AI投資ブーム下で企業の外部資金調達は拡大も、債務調達の増勢は限定的
 非金融法人企業は資金余剰が継続
― 設備投資が高水準で推移する一方、企業部門全体では潤沢な内部資金が投資を下支え
 AI投資ブームのもと企業の外部資金調達は拡大
― 押し上げの主因は株式発行。債務調達は一定の規模を維持しているものの増勢は鈍化。投資を主導するハイパース
ケーラーの高いキャッシュ創出力に加え、株式による資金調達を反映した格好
5

### Page 7

米国：債務調達は全般に減速。企業金融で存在感高めるプライベートクレジットも拡大一服
 債務調達の内訳をみると、社債、銀行借入、プライベートクレジットとも前期から減速
― とりわけプライベートクレジットは2024～25年の拡大局面から2026年入り後は鈍化が鮮明。IT分野では成約件数が大
幅に減少、全体に占めるシェアも低下。ソフトウェア企業を巡る信用リスクへの警戒や案件組成の減速が影響した模様
 減速したとはいえ、プライベートクレジットは企業金融で一定の存在感
― 企業債務残高に占めるプライベートクレジットの割合は7％程度と銀行借入にほぼ匹敵。過去10年間では3倍に拡大
6

### Page 8

米国：AI投資の外部化でプライベートクレジットを含む案件型金融が拡大する可能性
 事業開発会社（BDC）による資金供給の縮小が際立つ。足元ではプライベート・デットファンドを通じた供給にもかげり
― プライベートクレジットの活用が限定的だった背景には、信用力の高いハイパースケーラーによるデータセンターやAI
サーバー投資が中心だったことも一因
 AI関連投資ブームのすそ野拡大に伴いプライベートクレジットなどの活用余地が高まる可能性
―SPV・JV、データセンター事業者、電力・送配電・冷却など周辺インフラへ投資主体が広がれば、個別案件のキャッシュ
フローや資産価値に応じた資金調達需要が高まることも。案件型資金調達の活用余地拡大には目配り
7
プライベートクレジットの供給主体別増減
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 9

日本：中東情勢悪化を受けて輸送コストが急騰。原油高と合わせて輸入負担を押し上げ
 原油輸入額の増加で貿易赤字が拡大
―6月以降、割高な米国産を中心に原油の代替調達が進む中で輸入金額が増加
 中東情勢悪化で原油タンカー運賃が夏場以降急騰
― 運賃は7月末から約3倍に上昇。今後、割高な米国産から中東産へと回帰する中でも輸入負担は下がりにくい可能性
― 貿易赤字が長期化し、円安が進めば、輸入物価を一段と押し上げるリスク
8

（年／月）
日本：CPIはエネルギー補助効果で小幅鈍化も、補助終了後は再加速のリスク
9
 8月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋1.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と小幅に鈍化
― 電気・ガス料金への補助再開でエネルギー関連が下落。補助効果でCPIを0.2％Pt程度押し下げ
― 一方、既往の原油高に伴う最終財への価格転嫁が徐々に浸透するなか、ラップや洗剤等を中心にその他財価格が上昇
 こうした価格転嫁は今後も続くとみられるほか、電気・ガス料金の補助効果が剥落するもとで、エネルギー物価は再加速へ
― 原油高等が時間差を伴って電気・ガス料金に波及。補助効果が剥落する11月には、コアCPIを0.44％Pt程度押し上げへ

― また、原油価格の再上昇や輸送コスト上昇による輸入物価の上振れは、CPIの更なる押し上げ要因に
補助再開でエネルギー関連が下落
一方、原油高等の価格転嫁により、
その他財価格は上昇

日本：日銀追加利上げは最短インターバル。今後も日銀は3カ月に1回程度のペースで利上げへ
10
 日銀は9月会合で利上げを決定（反対2名）。利上げは6月以来3カ月ぶりで、今次局面では最も短いインターバル
―9月会合後の会見で植田総裁は、物価押し上げから物価上振れ抑制へと「政策の局面が変化した」と説明
― 政策金利は1.25％と、日銀による中立金利推計値のレンジ下限を上回るが、金融環境は利上げ後も緩和的と評価
 今後も日銀は3カ月に1回程度のペースで利上げを続ける可能性大
― 大幅利上げや連続利上げについては物価上振れリスクが大きい場合の選択肢と説明。急激な政策変更を回避する観点

から、先手を打って利上げを実施していく姿勢を強調
物価上振れリスクへの対応から
利上げ間隔が短期化
政策金利とブレークイーブン・インフレ
（注） ブレークイーブン・インフレは基調的物価上昇率の一面を捉えていると考えられるが、
必ずしも基調的物価上昇率そのものを示す指標ではない点に留意
（出所）日本銀行、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成 （出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
日銀9月会合のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
声明文
• 企業の賃金・価格設定行動の積極化や中長期的な予想物価上昇
率の上昇などを踏まえると、2％物価目標を上振れるリスクがある
• 企業間取引における価格上昇圧力が消費者段階にも波及し始め
ているほか、賃金上昇の販売価格への転嫁の動きも続いている
• 政策金利の変更後も、緩和的な金融環境は維持される
会見での注目発言
• 基調的物価上昇率が2％に接近し、政策局面が変化。物価上振れ
を回避する観点が重要に
• 原油価格の上昇やそこから波及した物価上昇圧力、AI関連需要

や円安進行が幅広い品目の物価上昇圧力につながる可能性
• 基調的物価の2％定着を確認するのにはしばらく時間がかかる
• 金融緩和の度合いは利上げにより少しずつ縮まっているが、銀行
貸し出しや資産市場は依然として緩和的
• 急激な利上げで金融環境が大きく変化することや、それによる経
済へのマイナス影響を回避する観点は重要
• 大幅利上げに追い込まれる可能性を減らすように政策を運営する
注目点②原油輸送費が急騰。貿易赤字長期化で円安圧力も

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6

師預期第三季標普500企業獲利年增約29.5%，加上企業獲利預期持續上修，強化投資人對高利率環境下企業基本面的信心。

• 實質薪資持續成長，內需復甦動能增強：日本8月實質現金收入年增1.5%，連續第8個月成長，名目工資與基本工資均年增

擴大，且企業治理改革、AI投資及自動化需求仍提供獲利支撐。近期日股AI與半導體族群已明顯走強，顯示市場仍重視企業
獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，美國財政部
中國預計將減少美元儲備

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.760，低點在 31.946，以 31.830 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方，市場預期聯準
會後續仍有升息的空間，提振美元指數表現，加以外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣走貶
至 31.900 上方；不過適逢月底也是季底，匯價逢高吸引出口商進場賣美金，緩解台
幣貶值的速度；臨近週末，美國 8 月核心 PCE 數據 3%低於預期 3.3%，但市場等待
9 月非農就業數據，交投偏向謹慎觀望，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.830。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略有
減弱，市場對於 10 月升息的預期下降，但仍預期 12 月會再度升息。美元指數雖然
脫離高點，但仍在 102 附近高位震盪，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台股再創收盤新高且外資轉為買超台股，且隨著 AI 需求持續帶動台灣經濟出口表
現強勁，預計出口商後續拋匯需求將持續提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，預估本週台幣
區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/5/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪偏升。隨著美中兩國完成新一輪談判，雙方同意延長休兵，從
原定的 11 月延長到明年 1 月 ， 降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險 ， 支撐人民幣的走勢由
6.7170 走升至 6.7030；不過由於美伊戰事持續 ，國際油價持穩於每桶 100 美元上方 ，
加以美元指數及美債收益率雙雙走高 ， 令人民幣由升轉貶至6.7200 上方 ； 臨近週末，
美國公布 9 月非農數據降溫，聯準會10 月升息預期下滑，美元指數回落，境外人民
幣走升至 6.7000 週內高點，終場境外人民幣收在 6.7061，週線升值 166 點，境內人
民幣於十一長假前夕收盤於在 6.7053，週線升值 72 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場來看 ， 美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬
人，遠低於市場預期 9 萬人，且失業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，顯示就業市場動能略
為減弱。數據公布後，市場對於聯準會10 月升息的預期下降，美元指數小幅回落，
有助於人民幣的表現；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價格再度升破 6.7400，有助
於人民幣的走勢 ， 不過10/1~10/7 是中國十一長假 ， 預計人民幣走勢主要將受國際美
元影響；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線仍向下發散，空頭格
局不變，不過匯價反彈至 20 日均線附近，顯示下行動能暫緩。綜合以上，預估本週
人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7300

美元兌日圓匯率走勢

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪上行。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，USD/JPY 大致
在 156.50 至 157.85 間波動；週間，適逢日本上半年會計年度結算，出口商拋匯使資
金匯回力道強勁 ， 帶動USD/JPY 下行最低來到 156.35，但逢低有買盤進場，USD/JPY
回到 157 整數關卡之上，後隨美債殖利率創高、美元指數突破 102 關卡，USD/JPY
上行最高來至 158.45；臨近週末，美國公布 9 月非農就業人數低於預期，USD/JPY
一度回落至 157 下方，終場收在 157.83，週線上漲 57 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪 ， 主要原因如下 ： 一 、日本 9 月製造業 PMI 由 54.9
回落至 54.1，增長速度放慢至六個月以來的低位，主因為產出和新訂單增長放緩，
經濟復甦力道可能減緩，不利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%，
美國 9 月非農數據 2.9 萬人均低於預期，通膨和勞動力市場略有降溫使市場對聯準
會升息預期下滑，加之部分聯準會官員表示無需急於進一步升息，Fedwatch 顯示 10
月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/5/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美元指數持穩在 101 以上高位，EUR/USD 震盪下
行至 1.1310，而在紐約聯儲主席威廉姆斯發表不急於再次升息的偏鴿言論後，
EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1340；週間，美國 8 月 PCE 數據低於預期，EUR/USD 一度反彈
至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.1300 關卡最低來至 1.1214；臨近週末，美國 9 月非農就業人數低
於預期，EUR/USD 反彈至 1.1285 後回落，終場收在 1.1253，週線下跌 138 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元地區消費者
物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

場資金供給仍充裕，惟需求疲弱，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投仍冷清，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三於處所成交在 1.82%，10 年
期指標券則未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價為 1.95%。 
第四季台債發行量雖偏少 ， 惟美伊和談依舊陷入僵局 ， 國際油價續處高位 ， 通
膨疑慮尚存，國際債市走勢仍偏空，預期台債殖利率短線將維持高檔盤整格
局，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.95%~2%。

美國財政壓力仍高，房市降溫與通膨回升增添經濟不確定性 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1,668 億美元，年減 51.6%，主因收入增
加、支出下降，但部分支出提前至7 月支付，影響單月表現。累計前11
個月赤字仍近 2 兆美元，關稅收入轉正，惟利息支出持續攀升，財政壓
力仍高。 
⚫ 8 月美國成屋銷售年率降至 398 萬戶，月減 2.0%、年減 1.2%，為 2025
年 6 月以來首度跌破 400 萬戶，顯示高房貸利率持續抑制房市需求。房
價中位數為 42.91 萬美元，年增 1.6%，連續 38 個月上漲；待售成屋庫
存增至 162 萬戶，相當於 4.9 個月供給量，創逾 10 年高點。整體而言，
成屋市場呈現銷售放緩、房價續升與庫存增加態勢，供給改善有助市場
逐步趨於平衡。 
⚫ 8 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%，高於上月 0.1%，年增率維持
3.4%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，年增率則降至 2.4%。能源價格由跌轉升，
其中汽油上漲 3.9%， 住房月增0.3%， 為推升通膨主因 ； 食品月增0.1%。
整體而言，短期物價壓力升溫，但核心通膨仍持續降溫。 
⚫ 8 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 0.4%、年增 5.4%，高於上月的 0.1%
及 4.8%，主要反映能源價格回升影響，其中柴油價格大漲24.1%；服務
價格則受運輸及倉儲成本增加推升而續呈上揚。核心 PPI 月增 0.3%，
低於前月的 0.4%，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍具韌性，惟基礎通膨動能尚
未明顯升溫。

一、 美國—財政壓力仍高，房市降溫與通膨回升增添經濟不確定性 
2026 年 8 月(2026 財年第 11 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1,668.0 億美元，
較去年同期 3,447.9 億美元縮減 51.6%；當月政府收入約 3,600.3 億美元，年增
4.6%，政府支出約 5,268.3 億美元，年減23.5%，惟部分原定 8 月初支付的軍人薪
資與退休金、退伍軍人福利、補充保障收入及部分醫療保險給付款項因 8 月 1 日
適逢非工作日而提前至 7 月支付，使單月支出受到付款時點影響。 收入方面，8
月關稅淨收入約 128.4 億美元，扭轉 7 月關稅淨收入為負 85.5 億美元的情況；累
計前 11 個月關稅淨收入約 1,673.1 億美元，較去年同期增加 1.3%。 支出方面，
8 月淨利息支出約 856.1 億美元，累計前 11 個月達約 1 兆 169.7 億美元，年增
8.9%，顯示利息負擔持續增加。 累計 2026 財年前 11 個月，美國聯邦政府財政
赤字約 1 兆 9,655.9 億美元，較去年同期小幅縮減 0.4%，其中收入年增 3.3%至約
4 兆 8,454.5 億美元，支出年增 2.2%至約 6 兆 8,110.4 億美元。整體而言，8 月單
月赤字雖明顯縮減 ， 但部分受到付款時點移轉影響 ， 且累計赤字仍接近2 兆美元 ，
加上利息支出持續攀升，美國聯邦財政壓力仍維持高檔。 
2026 年 8 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率降至 398 萬戶 ， 較上月減少2.0%，
較去年同期下降 1.2%，並為 2025 年 6 月以來首次跌破 400 萬戶，顯示高房貸利
率持續抑制房市交易動能。區域表現方面，西部銷售持平，東北部、中西部及南
部分別月減 4.0%、3.1%及 1.6%；房價方面，成屋售價中位數為42.91 萬美元，年
增 1.6%，連續 38 個月上漲。供給端則持續改善，待售成屋庫存增至 162 萬戶，
月增 3.2%、年增 5.9%，相當於 4.9 個月供給量，為近 10 多年來最高水準，住房
可負擔指數亦由去年同期的 101.2 升至 104.7。整體而言，8 月美國成屋市場呈現
銷售放緩、房價續漲及庫存增加的格局，高利率仍抑制成交動能，惟供給增加及
可負擔性改善，有助提升購屋者的選擇與議價空間，並促進市場逐步趨於平衡。 
2026 年 8 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，高於上月的
0.1%，年增率維持 3.4%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，高於前月的 0.2%，但年增率由
2.5%降至 2.4%。在細項方面，能源價格由跌轉升，能源指數月增 2.1%，其中汽
油價格上漲 3.9%，成為推升整體物價的主要因素；住房指數月增 0.3%，食品指

前月略為放緩，基礎通膨動能尚未進一步升溫，後續仍須持續關注能源價格波動
及服務成本變化對整體物價走勢之影響。 
近期美加貿易衝突持續升溫，雙方關稅報復行動已進一步擴大至非關稅措施
領域 。 加拿大自9 月 8 日起正式對約 200 億美元以上美國商品課徵 15%至 50%不
等的報復性關稅，涵蓋鋼鐵、鋁製品、家電、服飾及農業設備等產品，以回應美
國先前對加拿大商品加徵關稅措施 。 面對加拿大反制 ， 美國政府隨即宣布擴大限
制措施，除關稅措施外，將自 9 月 29 日起限制部分加拿大乳製品、酒類及機車
產品輸美，並限制加拿大企業參與部分美國政府採購標案，使雙方爭端由關稅戰
延伸至市場准入及政府採購領域。 加拿大總理 Mark Carney 表示，美國措施可
能衝擊加拿大關鍵產業發展，加方將持續採取對等回應措施。市場亦關注美方後
續可能進一步提高加拿大汽車、鋼鐵及相關零組件關稅，增添北美供應鏈及企業
投資規劃不確定性。整體而言，美加經貿關係短期內仍難見明顯緩和，後續發展
恐進一步增添北美及全球貿易前景不確定性。 
美國國會近期推進針對俄羅斯的新制裁法案，內容包括授權總統對俄羅斯原
油或天然氣主要購買國的進口商品課徵最高 100%關稅，以進一步限制俄羅斯能
源收入並加大對俄施壓 。 該法案已獲參議院以高票通過 ， 眾議院則預計展開審議 ，

二、歐元區—歐洲央行如期升息一碼，且仍有繼續升息的空間 
 歐元區第二季經濟成長季率終值由初值上修 0.2 個百分點至 0.6%，但上修主
因來自愛爾蘭經濟大幅成長 10.2%；若排除愛爾蘭，則歐元區第二季經濟成長季
率僅約 0.3%。從需求面觀察，第二季消費貢獻 0.2 個百分點，雖然通膨上升侵蝕
購買力，但薪資漲幅仍優於預期，加上高儲蓄率支持，使消費動能得以延續。其
中 ， 服務消費維持穩健成長 ， 耐久財支出亦明顯增加 。 另方面 ， 淨出口貢獻0.9 個
百分點，但設備投資下滑使整體固定投資表現仍顯疲弱。 
 歐洲央行 9 月 10 日如期宣布升息一碼，存款機制利率調升為 2.5%，但仍維
持「依經濟數據逐次決策」的立場。值得注意的是，本次歐洲央行分別上修2026
年、2027 年經濟成長率 0.1、0.2 個百分點至 0.9%、1.4%，以及 2028 年核心通膨
上修 0.1 個百分點至 2.3%， 反映歐洲央行認為經濟尚具韌性 ， 即便通膨壓力增溫 ，
仍能減輕其負面影響，使得政策利率繼續上調的空間，故一般預期歐洲央行將會
在 10 月政策會議採取觀望，並在12 月宣布升息一碼，2027 年第一季升息機率亦
有所上升，歐洲央行貨幣政策短期內仍將維持偏鷹立場。 
三、日本—內閣府上修第二季經濟成長，薪資動能續強 
2026 年第二季經濟成長率由 1.1%上修至 1.4%，主要受國內需求及庫存
貢獻改善帶動，其中以資本支出修正為國內需求上修的主因。展望下半年，
在政府財政刺激措施、企業持續擴大設備投資及薪資成長帶動消費支出的支
撐下，日本經濟可望維持溫和成長態勢，惟外部需求復甦力道、能源價格變
動及地緣政治風險仍為影響經濟表現的不確定因素。 
7 月薪資年增 4.7%，優於市場預期，其中夏季獎金年增 6.3%為主要推升
因素；經常性薪資及兼職時薪分別年增 4.1%及 5.7%，增速均較前期擴大 ， 顯
示薪資成長動能持續增強。此外，實質薪資年增3.4%，反映勞工購買力進一
步改善。在企業獲利表現穩健支撐下，儘管仍面臨成本上升及外部環境不確
定性 ， 企業仍維持獎金發放與加薪趨勢 ， 有助支撐民間消費韌性及經濟表現 。

### Page 8

用相關內容。

四、中國—進出口同步強勁成長，貿易順差增幅則相對有限 
8 月出口年增率較 7 月回升 1.1 個百分點至 25%，出口總值為 4,014 億美元；
累計前八個月出口年增率為 19.3%，累積出口總值為 29,238 億美元。前八個月
IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)累積出口額分別成長 103.9%、49.4%；船
舶累積出口成長 35.2%；汽車累積出口成長 53.2%。另方面，8 月進口年增率較 7
月回升 0.7 個百分點至 28.2%， 進口總值為2,824 億美元 ； 累計前八個月進口值成
長 28.2%至 21,183 億美元，其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)進口分別
成長 61.7%、105.1%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部分伺服器關鍵組件有賴
於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前八個月貿易順差達 8,055 億美元，僅較去年
同期增加 2.6%， 增幅明顯低於出口 ， 顯示出口擴張同時伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅
增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價值產品依賴國際供應鏈支
持。 
8 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 7 月的 3.5%回升至 3.8%，主因原油價格自 7 月
起走升，其影響逐漸反映於上游生產成本。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年
增率反彈 7.3 個百分點至 10.5%，並帶動燃料加工業通膨上揚，抵銷近期鋼鐵價
格下滑，導致相關礦產開採及冶煉加工產品價格較去年同期下滑的影響。此外，
對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學工業、化纖產業通膨沒有明顯變動，部分可能
與先前油價上漲期間累積較高庫存有關，惟在近期油價再度走升下，不排除相關
成本壓力於未來數月逐步反映。另因金價盤整，有色金屬礦採選業出廠價格年增
率亦持續放慢。 
8 月消費者通膨(CPI)回升 0.3 個百分點為 0.8%，但仍然是近七個月的次低水
準，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)回升 0.1 個百分點至 1 %。當月受國際原油價格上
漲影響，燃油通膨反彈 7.5 個百分點至 8.3%；手機零組件上漲，通信工具通膨上
揚 2.3 個百分點至 10.6%；夏季旅遊需求支持旅遊服務通膨增加 0.1 個百分點至
2.7%；其他商品及服務價格則大致平穩。整體而言，8 月能源價格有所回升，但
在企業庫存水位仍偏高情況下，抑制成本的傳導速度，除燃油價格外，尚未明顯
反映於其他消費項目通膨。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，若油價維持高檔或
進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。

五、印度及東南亞—油價上揚、通膨升溫，進口成本上漲恐使貿易收支惡化 
東南亞:(1)印尼：7 月出口額 262.2 億美元、年增 6.05%，非油氣產品出口年增
6.84%，礦物燃料、鋼鐵及電氣機械及設備出口增幅均逾 2 成，油氣產品出口
大幅下滑 14.58%；對美國（8.61%）、中國（17.52%）、日本（7.93%）、東
協（16.72%） 出口強勁成長。7 月進口年增 27.02%達 260.9 億美元，創歷史新

自美進口成長 13.1%。進出口相抵，7 月貿易收支意外轉為順差 1.3 億美元。
印尼經濟較依賴進口 ， 部分進口需求係配合國內投資及生產活動擴張 ，另國際
油價上漲增加能源進口負擔，在出口成長幅度相對有限情形下 ， 進口持續快速
增加將壓縮商品貿易順差，並可能進一步擴大經常帳赤字。8 月消費者物價指
數(CPI)年增率升至 3.19%，主要因食品、飲料和香菸價格上漲，以及個人照護
和交通成本增加所致 ，剔除波動較大的食品和政府管制價格的核心通膨率升至
2.92%，為 2023 年 3 月以來最高。儘管通膨有所回升，但仍維持在印尼央行
1.5%至 3.5%的目標區間內。(2)泰國：8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率上升至
2.53%，主要因中東衝突導致油價上漲，進而推高國內燃料價格所致，食品、
非食品價格通膨分別上升 2.99%、2.23%，剔除能源及生鮮食品的核心通膨率
上揚至 1.44%，創 3 年多新高，泰國商務部維持其 2026 年整體通膨預期在
1.5%-2.5%之間，通膨水準仍處於泰國央行 1%-3%的目標區間內。(3)菲律賓：
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率從 7 月的 6.2%降至 6.1%， 主要因食品/非酒精
飲料、房屋/公用事業價格漲幅放緩，惟受燃油價格上漲影響，交通運輸價格

力漸增。(4)越南: 8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率上升至 4.89%，創 3 個月新

需求及中國供應鏈移轉，帶動電子產品出口，但主要集中於外資企業，資本財

五、台灣—通膨略降溫，AI 需求支撐出口成長 
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.04%，雖較前兩月回落，但已連續 4 個月高
於 2%的通膨警戒線，主要受國際油價上漲帶動油料費、機票及旅遊團費增加，
加上外食費、房租及電費等價格上漲所致；惟蔬菜價格因去年基期較高而大幅下
跌，抵銷部分漲幅。整體而言，通膨壓力雖略有降溫，但核心 CPI 仍達 2.30%，
顯示外食、房租等服務類價格仍具黏著性，物價上漲壓力尚未完全消退。 
 8 月出口達 824 億美元，年增 41%，續創歷年單月新高，主要受惠於人工智
慧應用快速擴展及國際雲端服務業者持續加大資本支出，帶動半導體、伺服器及
相關資通訊產品需求強勁。雖然部分傳統產業仍受全球景氣復甦步調不一及貿易
環境不確定性影響，但科技產品需求熱絡使整體出口表現優於預期。展望未來，
在 AI、 高效能運算及雲端服務需求持續成長 ， 以及第四季傳統外銷旺季來臨帶動
下，我國出口動能可望延續，惟仍須關注地緣政治風險及國際貿易政策變化對外
需之影響。

事项：
9 月份，新增社会融资规模 3.55万亿元，同比多增 192亿元；新增人民币贷款 1.08万亿元，同比少增 2100
亿元。
评论：
图1：金融数据分项一览
资料来源：Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
结论：9 月金融数据总体好于市场预期，开年后持续走弱的局面有所缓和。但结构上，政府债券支撑社融
季末回升，非政府部门融资需求仍然较弱。社融新增 3.55万亿元、同比多增 192亿元；信贷新增 1.08 万
亿元，但同比仍少增 2100 亿元。企业中长贷是贷款端主要支撑，居民端暂时停止缩表；M1回落至 3.2%、
与 M2的剪刀差重新走阔至 4.0 个百分点；财政资金投放加快，9 月财政缺口预计冲高。值得注意的是，新
型政策性金融工具开始落地，9月投放规模或许显著超过部分测算，达 3000亿左右。
展望后续，10 月低基数下金融数据同比读数有望延续改善。去年 10月新增贷款 2200 亿元、新增社融 8178
亿元，基数偏低。四季度新型政策性金融工具 9 月启动首批投放后将持续发力，叠加近期的购房贷款贴息

政策，对企业中长期贷款和居民按揭能构成一定支撑。但在实体融资需求实质性回暖之前，贷款余额与社
融存量同比的下行趋势将会持续。
具体看，9 月数据：

### Page 2

社融季末回升，主要靠政府债支撑。9 月新增社融 3.55万亿元，同比多增 192 亿元；政府债券净融资 1.53
万亿元、同比多增 3438亿元，是同比转正的主要支撑；扣除政府债券的非政府融资同比少增 3246亿元，
显示实体融资需求仍然较弱。此外，当月新型政策性金融工具集中落地，交叉比对公开信息发现，其投放
规模或达 3000亿元左右（其中直接公布的约 1000亿元为民间投资），即非政府融资中亦含有政策驱动的
成分。
信贷季末回升超预期，居民端停止缩表。9 月新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿，高于市场预期，同比少增 2100
亿元，贷款余额增速降至 4.8%、续创新低；企业中长贷是主要支撑，居民贷款净增为零，结束了连续两个
月的缩表。
总体来看，信用扩张中枢下移的趋势尚未扭转。政府债券融资放量叠加财政存款下降，财政资金季末投放
加快；受去年同期基数效应影响，M1 增速回落至 3.2%、与 M2 的剪刀差重新走阔。10 月低基数下同比读数
有望改善，随着政策效果逐步显现，四季度信贷有望边际修复。

解读：
 社融：政府债为主要支撑，非政府融资仍弱
9 月新增社融 3.55万亿元，较 8 月的 1.66 万亿元明显回升，同比多增 192 亿元，基本持平于过去五年同
期约 3.57万亿元的均值；月末社融存量同比增长 7.1%，较上月回落 0.1个百分点，继续创 2003年有数据
以来的新低。结构上，政府债券净融资 1.53万亿元，占新增社融的 43.1%，同比多增 3438亿元，贡献度
达 1791%，是社融同比转正的主要支撑。9 月是社融季节性回升月份，当月环比增加约 1.89万亿元，明显
高于过去五年同期平均的约 0.74万亿元。各科目对同比增量的贡献排序：政府债券（+3438亿元）、外币
贷款（+478亿元）、直接融资（-402亿元）、非标（-1102亿元）、人民币贷款（-3342 亿元）
1
。

 信贷：季末回升超预期，居民端停止缩表
信贷季末回升，高于市场预期。9月新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿元，高于市场普遍预期的约 9900亿元，但

低于过去五年同期约 1.86 万亿元的均值，同比少增 2100亿元；9 月末贷款余额同比增长 4.8%，较上月回
落 0.1个百分点，继续创有数据以来新低。
居民端暂时停止缩表。9 月居民部门贷款净增为零，结束 7-8 月连续两个月的净减少：短期贷款减少 900
亿元、中长期贷款增加 874 亿元，分别同比少增 2321亿元和 1626亿元；9 月末落地的房贷贴息政策对按

 非标、直接融资与政府债券

2 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Commodities: From Inflation Hedge to Structural Growth Asset
Anna Snider, Managing Director and Head of Investment Manager Selection
(Due Diligence)
A broader role in portfolios: For decades, commodities occupied a relatively narrow
role in portfolios: protection against inflation when rising prices eroded the value
of traditional assets. That function remains important, but the investment case is

broadening. Commodities now sit at the intersection of geopolitical fragmentation,
supply-chain security, reshoring, inventory rebuilding, and the buildout of AI and digital
infrastructure. Together, these forces are turning parts of the asset class from purely
cyclical exposures into potential long-term growth beneficiaries.
The diversification case is also becoming more relevant. In Q1, when inflation concerns
peaked, the Bloomberg Commodity Index returned close to 25%, outperforming both

Equities and Fixed Income. At the same time, the relationship among major asset
classes has shifted. Equity and Fixed Income correlations have tightened in recent years,
while commodities have moved in the opposite direction: over the trailing five years,
their correlation with both Equities and bonds has been negative. That combination of
inflation sensitivity and differentiated return behavior can make commodities useful
beyond a simple tactical hedge.

AI infrastructure is a physical buildout: AI is often viewed through semiconductors,
software platforms, and hyperscale data centers. Yet the infrastructure required to
deploy AI is extraordinarily commodity intensive. It requires continuous electricity,
generation capacity, transmission lines, substations, transformers, cooling systems,
networking hardware and structural materials. In that sense, AI is not only a computing
revolution. It is also an energy, infrastructure, and critical-materials investment cycle.

Power is the first constraint. Data centers require reliable, around-the-clock electricity.
Natural gas remains a practical source of incremental generation in the U.S., while
nuclear power is emerging as a strategic long-term option for hyperscalers seeking
stable baseload supply. Solar and wind can add capacity and support decarbonization
goals, but grid reliability and storage become more important as power demand rises.
That supports investment in batteries, backup power and grid-scale storage, with lithium

and graphite among the relevant inputs.
The buildout also increases demand for industrial metals. Copper is a clear beneficiary
because it is essential to transmission, transformers, cooling systems and data-center
wiring. Aluminum and iron ore are used in facilities, electrical equipment, transmission
networks and supporting infrastructure. Semiconductor fabrication adds another layer
of demand through industrial gases such as helium and neon, high-purity quartz for

wafers and optics, and gallium and germanium for advanced chips, photonics, and
telecommunications. AI-enabled automation and robotics also depend on permanent
magnets that use rare earth elements, including neodymium-praseodymium and
dysprosium-terbium.
Demand has moved faster than supply: This demand surge resembles earlier cycles
in semiconductors and memory, where expectations pulled spending forward. Metals
and mining companies are already seeing earnings growth and margin expansion,

yet the industry has struggled to keep pace with demand. The imbalance matters
because commodity supply is slow to respond. Production of many strategic materials
is geographically concentrated, permitting timelines are lengthy, recycling capacity is
limited, and substitution can remain years from commercial viability. New mines and
processing facilities can take a decade or more to reach production.
Investment Implications
Commodities may increasingly serve

three portfolio purposes: inflation
protection, diversification from
traditional assets, and participation in
long-term infrastructure and resource-
demand growth, supporting a diversified
allocation across direct commodities
and select producer Equities rather than
a single directional trade.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Governments are also taking a larger role through industrial policy, export controls,
stockpiling and efforts to secure critical supply chains. Restrictions involving gallium,
germanium, graphite and other strategic materials underscore how geopolitics can affect
availability and pricing. As a result, the current cycle may produce greater scarcity and
pricing power than demand growth alone would suggest.

So far, this dynamic has been most visible in industrial metals, but the same forces could
extend into agriculture. Reshoring changes where food and inputs are produced and
processed, while inventory rebuilding can lift demand for fertilizer, fuel, machinery and
storage. Agriculture has not yet experienced the same earnings and pricing impact as
metals, but supply-chain resilience could make it a broader beneficiary over time.
Cyclical forces still matter: The structural thesis does not eliminate near-term

volatility. Energy remains a key transmission mechanism for inflation because oil and
natural gas underpin transportation, manufacturing and industrial production. Geopolitical
disruptions can cascade through supply chains, raising costs and moving both commodity
futures and spot prices. Currency dynamics matter as well. A stronger U.S. dollar can
restrain commodity prices by increasing costs for non-U.S. buyers, while renewed dollar
weakness could support emerging-market demand and reprice dollar-denominated

assets.
A stabilization of geopolitical tensions could create a more constructive cyclical backdrop
through lower energy volatility, improved business confidence and stronger capital
investment. Conversely, renewed conflict can produce sharp and uneven moves across
the complex. These crosscurrents reinforce the value of treating commodities as a
diversified allocation rather than a single directional trade.
Gold, implementation and the portfolio case: Gold is fundamentally different from

most industrial commodities, but it deserves attention. Its traditional relationships with
interest rates and the dollar have been less reliable lately. Even so, sustained central bank
and exchange-traded fund buying points to durable long-term demand despite bouts of
volatility such as the selloff earlier this year. Gold also tended to move inversely with oil
during the height of the Iran conflict, illustrating how different commodities can respond

differently to the same geopolitical shock.
Investors can access the theme through commodity futures or producer Equities. Direct
commodity exposure is generally the clearer vehicle for inflation protection, scarcity, and
geopolitical supply shocks. Producer equities can offer greater upside when commodity
prices rise faster than operating costs because earnings and margins expand, but that
leverage is asymmetric. Company-specific execution, financing, cost and valuation risks

can still cause Equities to underperform the underlying commodity. A blended approach
may capture both physical scarcity and corporate operating leverage.
Ultimately, commodities remain sensitive to energy markets, currencies, growth, and
geopolitics, but their portfolio role is expanding. They can provide inflation protection and
diversification while offering exposure to AI infrastructure, electrification, energy security,
defense modernization, reshoring and, potentially, agricultural supply-chain investment.

The strongest beneficiaries of the AI trade may therefore extend well beyond technology
companies to the materials and power systems that make deployment possible.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Rising Global Interest Rates and International Markets
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Strong earnings growth has so far delivered a fourth consecutive year of double-digit
gains for global Equity indexes in 2026, propelling them to new all-time highs. But a
growing risk throughout the year has been the increase in government bond yields, which

have also broken out to multi-decade highs across a number of the major markets. The
U.S. Treasury will this week begin its expanded Treasury securities buyback program
announced in mid-August in response to rising rates. But 10-year yields across all
the major G10 countries have moved higher, in many cases by as much as or more
than in the U.S. market. Against a backdrop of above-trend growth in nominal gross
domestic product (GDP), high energy prices and elevated inflation, investors appear to

be reassessing monetary policy expectations, particularly in Europe where most of the
2026 increase in 10-year yields has been driven by inflation breakevens (Exhibit 1). Fixed
Income markets are consequently looking for policy rate hikes from the European Central
Bank later this week and potentially from the Bank of Japan later next month.
The rise in global interest rates has also come alongside an expansion in fiscal policy
across the major markets. In Europe, 2026 and 2027 are expected to be the peak

spending years for defense and infrastructure expenditure plans unveiled last year. The
so-called Readiness 2030 package allocates €800 billion to increased European Union
defense spending as it looks to move toward its 5% of GDP target by 2035. And at the
same time, the German government relaxation of its national debt brake has directed
€500 billion toward funding for investment in clean energy, transportation and digital
infrastructure. Similarly in Japan, the government supermajority is looking to enact a

new stimulus aimed at raising investment levels across 17 strategic sectors such as
semiconductors, defense and shipbuilding, in addition to a planned two-year cut to the
consumption tax on food from April 2027. And in the UK, the new central government
leadership is also likely to announce new spending measures when its first national
budget is released next month. To the extent that increased public investment spending
can lift productivity and support nominal GDP growth in Europe and Japan, the rise in

yields need not undermine economic growth or derail the equity market advance. But
higher rates, especially in response to supply-driven energy price inflation, nonetheless
remain a risk for developed market equity returns going into the end of the year.
Exhibit 1: Global Bond Yields in Major Markets have Risen by as Much or More
than U.S. Yields in 2026.

For many emerging markets the picture looks quite different. In contrast with the U.S.,
Japan and Europe, inflation rates are generally well below central bank targets outside
a limited number of resource exporters in Latin America and the booming AI-linked
economies of Korea and Taiwan (Exhibit 2). As a result, policy rate expectations over

the coming quarters are stable or lower for several emerging markets (including China,
Malaysia, Thailand, Poland, Brazil and Mexico) according to Bloomberg consensus forecasts.
Investment Implications
Ongoing strength in economic growth
and corporate earnings should support
global equity markets even in the face
of rising rates. For Emerging Markets
(EM) Equities in particular, contained
inflation, flat to lower local policy rate
expectations and stronger fundamentals

should limit the impact. And we
continue to favor EM Equities over
International Developed Markets, where
inflation levels are generally still above
target, central banks are expected
to raise rates and investors will be
watching for fiscal sustainability as
governments implement expansionary
budget plans.

Historically, rising U.S. interest rates have been a major headwind for emerging markets.
But looking ahead in the current cycle, we still expect the impact of potential Federal
Reserve (Fed) rate hikes to be lower than in the past. Over recent years, the association
between tighter Fed policy and weaker EM returns appears to have diminished. EM
Equities for example were resilient in the face of aggressive U.S. rate increases in 2022
and 2023, broadly keeping pace with the rest of the world. This is in sharp contrast to

prior cycles in which tighter Fed policy has resulted in capital flight, deleveraging and
falling asset values across the emerging world. Particularly during the crisis periods of
the 1980s and 1990s—but also more recently during the 2015 taper tantrum—U.S.
monetary tightening has tended to attract capital away from riskier emerging countries
(particularly those with large current account deficits), reducing support for local asset
prices and exchange rates.

Associated strength in the U.S. dollar has typically been an additional hurdle. For those
emerging economies with fixed exchange rates, a loss of export competitiveness has
further undermined current account positions, forcing central banks to run down their
foreign exchange reserves. And where exchange rates are floating, foreign currency
debts have risen in local currency terms, resulting in widespread defaults, debt
downgrades, recessions and additional exchange rate depreciation.

But starting in the years that followed the 1997-1998 Asian financial crisis and
continuing into the current cycle, the fundamentals across much of the EM universe have
improved. Fixed exchange rates have been abandoned. EM foreign currency reserves have
roughly doubled as a share of GDP . The share of EM debt denominated in U.S. dollars
has fallen significantly, led by the private sector across households and corporates. And
for most countries, current account deficits have narrowed or disappeared entirely. As a

result, emerging markets have become much more insulated from the direction of U.S.
interest rates on a structural basis, and this has only been reinforced by the AI-driven
revaluation of key Asian markets in 2026. Greater concentration in Korea and Taiwan has
increased the EM weighting in the surplus Asia-Pacific region to 83% as at the end of
August—up from 81% at the start of the year and 71% a decade ago.
Above-trend growth in nominal GDP and ongoing strength in corporate earnings should

support global equity markets even in the face of rising interest rates. But for emerging
Equities in particular, contained inflation relative to central bank targets, flat to lower
local policy rate expectations and stronger economic fundamentals should limit the
impact from higher global rates. We continue to favor EM Equities over International
Developed Markets, where inflation levels are generally still above target, central banks
are expected to raise interest rates and investors will be watching for fiscal sustainability

as governments implement expansionary budget plans.

6 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The One-Offs Inflating Q2 Earnings
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Headline Q2 earnings numbers look almost too good to be true. And in a sense, they are.
With nearly all of S&P 500 companies’ results reported, EPS growth is tracking around
50% year-over-year (YoY)—not just an acceleration from Q1 but a second consecutive
quarter above 20%. Chiefly skewing the headline number are massive mark-to-market

gains on private investments for a handful of mega cap technology companies like
Alphabet and Amazon that booked outsized non-operating gains tied to their stakes in
Anthropic, while Microsoft benefited from its OpenAI investment. Strip those out, and
adjusted EPS growth notches down to 31%. The point being, after being sliced and
diced, 30%+ is well above any long-run historical earnings growth rate.
The one-off nature of earnings strength wasn’t limited to investment gains. A second,

less-discussed boost was tariff refunds. As Exhibit 3 illustrates, revenues from tariffs
steadily climbed from early 2025 through mid-2026 until the International Emergency
Economic Powers Act (IEEPA) tariff ruling which initiated the refund process. As
refunds were disbursed, customs receipts turned net negative in June and July, with
refund payments exceeding total tariff collections. Customs and Border Protection cash
withdrawals of more than $100 billion in refunds have now been paid—60% of the $165

billion in total eligible claims.
The recipients: the “importers of record,” or the dozens of S&P 500 companies that
have disclosed billions in refunds over the past two quarters; Apple alone received $2.19
billion, adding $0.11 to per-share earnings to their quarter. Other disclosed refunds
include Nike with $986 million, Amazon with $640 million, and General Motors refunded
$500 million (although accounting treatment and use of proceeds has varied).

The boost of investment gains and/or refunds may not be repeatable in future quarters.
Perhaps more telling is that the median company’s revenue grew roughly 15% YoY, the
strongest gain in years, corroborating that there is operating momentum rather than
just an accounting mirage for Q2 earnings. Margins, too, as a percent of GDP are at
their highest back to 1950, hovering north of 22%, see Exhibit 4. That this is occurring
amid rising rates and at a time of geopolitical uncertainty/disruption, rather than a post-

recessionary recovery, makes the results that much more impressive.
The Path Forward: Questions remain about the sustainability of these elevated growth
rates, although consensus expects EPS growth to stay above 20% in both Q3 and Q4.
If achieved, every quarter this year would see 20%-plus EPS growth. On the back of
brighter outlooks from Corporate America in recent weeks, full-year earnings growth
will decelerate into the mid-teens in 2027, but that moderation comes off a high base

of 2026. For now, the breadth of earnings growth is the encouraging story as 10 of
the 11 sectors reported growth (Energy and Communication Services leading the way,
Healthcare the only detractor). That’s a reflection of strong core businesses growing
revenues into 2027’s tougher YoY comparisons.
Investment Implications
Underlying earnings-per-share (EPS)
growth of around 30%, stripped
of investment gains, is historically
exceptional. It remains that robust

corporate earnings and positive
revisions have helped Equities absorb
concerns around the recent rise
in yields.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.

Document 234: First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#consumer #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ household incomes, assets and savings
update - June quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:24 October 08 2026 Share
New Zealand incomes and savings are rising, as is the value of households’ financial
assets. However, the value of housing assets remains flat.
Key takeouts from Stats NZ’s latest update on household incomes, assets and savings (June quarter
2026)

### Page 2

Household incomes
Disposable income levels in the economy grew by 5.2% in the year to June, down from 6.5% in the
year to March

Savings and wealth
Household savings have continued to rise, climbing by $2.5b over the past quarter.

Overall household wealth levels were up 1.9% over the past year. Under the surface, the make-up of
household wealth continues to shift.

Interest costs 
Overall household income levels are continuing to rise. Much of that is due to increases in
entrepreneurial earnings (which is in part due to increased earnings in the agricultural sector). Wage
and salary growth has been more limited but has picked up.
Household savings levels have been rising for three years now, with another solid rise over the past
three months. 
Overall household wealth levels have risen modestly. Although the value of households’ financial
assets is continuing to rise, housing assets have essentially shown no growth since late 2023.
Household spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter. That’s the first increase in
two years. The easing in average borrowing costs seen over the past few years has come to a close
with the RBNZ’s hiking cycle now in train.
Underpinning the rise in overall income levels have been continued increases in entrepreneurial
earnings, which are up 12% over the past year. That will include stronger earnings in the agricultural
sector, which has been boosted by the strength in commodity export prices. That lift in agricultural
earnings has been reflected in stronger economic confidence and spending in many rural regions.
We expect commodity export earnings will remain firm over the year ahead. 
In contrast, wage and salary earnings are up just 2.7% over the past year. That’s up from 1.8% in the
year to March, but it’s a fairly muted rise, especially given the continued increases in the population
and inflation of 4.1%.
June’s large increase in savings builds on the solid increases seen over the past three years.
The savings rate in the June quarter was 3.7% of disposable incomes, down just slightly from last
quarter.
The value of households’ financial assets has continued to rise and is up 4.6% over the past year. 
However, the value of housing and land assets is down 0.9% over the past year, and it has remained
essentially unchanged since late 2023. We expect housing market conditions will remain soft over
the coming year, limiting increases in overall household wealth. 
Households’ spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter, climbing by 0.3%.
While that’s a relatively small change, it is the first increase that we’ve seen in two years.

### Page 3

Related articles

Media Contact:
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist
P: +64 9 336 5668
M: +64 21 710 852
Over the past few years, many borrowers saw their debt servicing costs fall as they rolled off earlier
high mortgage rates and onto lower ones. That helped to support household spending.
However, the easing in average borrowing cost has now ended. Recent months have seen mortgage
rates pushing higher as the RBNZ has begun to hike the Official Cash Rate, with further hikes
expected over the coming year. 
Looking ahead, we don’t expect a significant increase in average household borrowing costs.
However, we won’t see the same support for spending that we did over the past few years.

Largan Precision
Core weakness outweighs progress in AI optics
CPO progress is more tangible but substantial success is already priced in
We maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target price as erosion in the core smartphone
business outweighs CPO progress. The Q326 call confirmed that Largan's fiber array has
passed customer qualification although its customer qualified another vendor's prism
micro-lens assembly (PMLA) that Largan would now buy in. If Largan qualifies the full

FAU plus carrier assembly for 2H27, content rises but with more pass-through cost and
less margin upside. We roll our valuation to 2028E, the first full year of CPO production,
though 25% below the NT$6,660 close on October 8.
Full FAU assembly would lift content, but with more pass-through
We believe Largan's FA customer is one of two  module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27
program, which we estimate could reach 40% share. The program is still subject to

successful yield ramp and could move to metalens in 2028, which would reopen supplier
qualification. Assembling the full FAU could lift Largan's ASP to US$125-150 leveraging
its precision alignment and automation but the PMLA, V-groove and carrier parts would
be pass through that cuts into margin leverage. We expand our FAU TAM analysis
(Figure 9S u m m a r y  o f  F A U  d r i v e r s :  1 2 . 3 m n / 3 5 . 6 m n  b a s e  c a s e  i n  2 0 2 8 - 2 0 3 0 ) with FAU market in 2028/30 expanding to 12.3mn/35.6mn units, with Largan

base case set at 25% of the TAM.
Core smartphone business stays soft into 2027
Q326 sales of NT$15.7bn, +15% QoQ/-11% YoY missedUBSe/Street +36%/+33% on
weaker flagship smartphone demand. GMs fell 7.2ppts QoQ to 42.2%, lowest in 13
years on renewable energy costs and low variable-aperture yields, dropping OpM to 17
year lows at 30.3% and EPS to NT$39.6, below UBS/street NT$51.50/NT$49.69. We cut
Q4 to NT$16.7bn +7% QoQ, 11% below consensus as its customer trimmed orders

after a pricier iPhone 18 launch and possible lag to Genius which is picking up periscope
share and outgrew in Q3 (+1% YoY vs. -11% YoY). We cut 2027 from NT$77bn to NT
$67bn, before a recovery to NT$79bn in 2028 with the next periscope upgrade. We see
GM recovering to 47.5%/48.9% in 2027-28 as yields improve and energy costs pass
through in 2028 pricing.
Maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target
We cut 2026E/27E EPS by 14%/16% to NT$160/NT$180 and introduce 2028E EPS of

NT$220 for the core smartphone business. We value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x
2028E ex-CPO EPS of NT$220, the middle of Largan's 13–18x historical range) and CPO
option at NT$1,700 or US$7bn (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35, near the
top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-unit, US
$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. That credits it with
yields and margins it still needs to prove in a quarter when yields and energy costs cut

7.2ppts from its lens margin. At ~NT$6,660, the shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO
EPS, about twice our base case.

Core smartphone lens seeing weakness with declining
margins
Q326 sales was reported light at NT$15.7bn, +15% QoQ missing UBSe/Street +36%/
+33% amid weaker flagship smartphone demand. GMs fell 720bp to 42.2%, lowest in
13 years on renewable energy costs and low variable-aperture yields, dropping OpM to
17 year lows at 30.3% and EPS to NT$39.6, below UBS/street NT$51.50/NT$49.69.
Renewable-energy costs are structural under Largan's long-term power commitments

and rise with Apple's share of the mix; with 2027 pricing set, pass-through is more likely
in 2028.
We cut Q4 to NT$16.7bn +7% QoQ, 11% below consensus as its customer trimmed
orders after a pricier iPhone 18 launch and possible lag to Genius which is picking up
periscope share and outgrew in Q3 (flat vs. -11% YoY).  October revenue should be flat
MoM and November lower as the key customer has shortened its forecast after cutting
flagship orders in September. The spring-2027 model starts shipping in October but will

not offset the flagship decline.

We cut 2026E/27E revenue 9%/13% to NT$61.6bn/NT$67.4bn and EPS 14%/16% to
NT$163/NT$180, and introduce 2028E revenue of NT$79.5bn, +18% YoY for the new

value separately under base, bull and bear scenarios. Including our base case, 2008E EPS
would be NT$255, 6% above the NT$240 consensus which likely includes some CPO.
We see GM recovering to 47.5%/48.9% in 2027-28 as yields improve and energy costs

pass-through in 2028 pricing. With a still a range of outcomes on 2028 CPO, we keep it
in our valuation as a scenario for our upside/downside/base cases.

CPO fiber array qualified, but rising pass-through as it upgrades to FAU
production
Largan's fiber array (FA) has passed qualification with a volume customer, and
management expects mass-production capacity by mid-2027 in a newly completed fab
dedicated to FA/FAU, using in-house equipment. The company's next step is to produce
the full detachable FAU mounted on a carrier, a board with three alignment beams that
sits on the optical engine's receptor. Largan can build FAUs now and says the customer

### Page 3

Largan Precision 12 October 2026 ab 3
would prefer it to supply the full assembly, but qualification is pending with the
customer preferring FAU and carrier sampled separately.
Even as its potential to supply the full FAU improve, Largan will buy in most of the
content. The customer has qualified another PMLA vendor and prefers semiconductor-
made optics; V-grooves and carrier beams and boards are outsourced and the customer
does not want Largan to make MT ferrules. Management described its role as closer to a

contractor and said margins hinge on yields, as the FA carries a high ASP but also a large
bill of materials.  Supplying the complete FAU could raise billed content, but a larger
purchased bill of materials also dilutes the relationship between revenue and value
added. Our full-FAU ASP assumption requires Largan to capture that invoice scope.
Next year's product is grating-coupled and works for both CPO and NPO while edge-
coupled designs are being explored with the customer. The current product does not go

directly to end customers, but management expects to work directly with end customers
on next-generation designs (likely 2028) as it progresses toward multi-row FAs.
To ramp production, Largan has bought several land parcels and will build new fabs from
scratch, with construction finishing around 2030. It may outsource some capacity in the
interim and said it cannot yet be sure capacity will meet demand.
Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes and even mix
of these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s
ASICs using 6.4Tb/s optical engines.

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030, 55% of 1.1mn 204.8T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.

Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra
and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We

assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market
opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028 and 8.9mn in 2030.

4. Non NVIDIA scale-up opportunity
Non-NVIDIA scale-up uses accelerator rack shipments x optical attach rate x FAUs per
rack, producing about 0.5m units in 2028 and 3.7m in 2030.

Overall market opportunity: 12.3mn/35.6mn generates a US$1.85bn/US
$4.5bn TAM in 2028/2030
We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native

NVIDIA GPUs and non-NVIDIA scale-up.  We add 10%/5% for FAUs lost at downstream
attach and test and for field spares, driving a market size of in our base case of 12.3mn
FAUs in 2028 and 35.6mn in 2030. At US$150/US$125 per FAU, that is a US$1.85bn
market in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 (bear/bull US$0.5bn/US$3.5bn in 2028; US
$1.4bn/US$9.0bn in 2030). The expansion to 2030 comes from wider optical adoption
and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline from US

$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Largan's CPO opportunity
Our base case gives Largan the benefit of the doubt for successfully ramping up a full
FAU production and capturing 25% of the overall FAU TAM, yielding NT$35 of 2028E
EPS, about 16% of our 2028E lens EPS. A 25% share means 3.1mn FAUs in its first full
year of production: about 29% of NVIDIA-linked units and roughly three-quarters of the

allocation our checks suggest its module partner holds. We book these at the full US
$150 FAU price and 50% gross margin, allowing for an early-supply premium even
though the optics, V-grooves and carrier parts are bought in. That gives 2028E CPO sales
of US$462mn, rising to US$1.1bn and NT$84 of EPS in 2030 on a larger market.
Our bear case (15% share, 45% GM) gives NT$5 of CPO EPS in 2028E and NT$14 in
2030E; our bull case (35% share, 55% GM) gives NT$103 and NT$267. Each 5ppt of

share moves 2028E CPO EPS by ~NT$6.9 (~NT$340/share at 49x), and each 5ppt of
gross margin by ~NT$4.6 (~NT$225/share).  If gross margin were 35% rather than 50%,
holding operating expenses at 12% of sales, EPS would fall to approximately NT$21. A
next-generation shift in lens or coupling design could also change content and
alignment requirements including a potential metalens design which could invite in
different suppliers.
Our 2030 base case implies ~8.9mn Largan FAUs a year (21mn in the upside case),

though with risks around competition in a crowded field, yields and technology. A shift
to semiconductor-made optics or metalens would loosen alignment tolerances and
erode the precision premium behind Largan's pitch. The upside lies in direct work with
cloud providers on multi-row FAs from 2028, where Largan's multi-layer alignment is
most differentiated.

Valuation and investment implications
We roll our valuation forward to 2028E and value Largan on a sum of the parts. We

value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x 2028E EPS, the middle of Largan's 13–18x
historical range) and CPO at NT$1,700 (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35,
near the top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-
unit, US$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. The 49x
multiple also assigns a substantial value to a business whose complete assembly still
requires qualification and whose margins still need to demonstrate can reach similar

levels to its mature smartphone business. At ~NT$6,660 recent share price level, the
shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO EPS, about twice our base case.
Upside risks include faster full-FAU qualification, firm volume commitments from
additional customers, stronger lens demand and better yields. Near-term catalysts are
the customer's capacity forecast and its visit to the new fab in the coming month. A
large forecast or faster FAU plus carrier qualification would be upside risks to our sell.

Forecast excess return -30.0%
Company Description
Founded in 1987, Largan Precision (Largan) is one of the world's leading manufacturers of
lenses used in cameras and IT products. It specialises in plastic/hybrid lenses and, to a lesser
extent, glass lenses and modules. In 2022, 20MP+ camera lenses were 10-20% of Largan's

shipment mix, 10MP lenses were 50-60%, 8MP lenses were 0-10% and other lenses were
30-40%.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business.
We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from smartphone OEM
clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a slower-than-
expected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success below the

market's elevated expectations.
We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-expected CPO qualification process at Largan.
In addition, if the company demonstrates exceptionally efficient and highly accurate
automation capabilities, it could support greater market share gains within the industry than
we currently anticipate.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.
